The files I generate don't have a character encoding specified and it leads to garbled text in kiwix-desktop:\n\n<img width=\"951\" height=\"237\" alt=\"Image\" src=\"https://github.com/user-attachments/assets/7501fd2b-f4b9-48cc-8511-2a6553d009d9\" />\n\n<details>\n\n<summary>html source</summary>\n\n```html\n<!DOCTYPE html>\n<html lang=\"en\" dir=\"ltr\"><head>\n    \n    \n \n    \n    \n     \n    \n    \n    \n    \n    \n    \n     \n    \n    \n    \n    \n    \n    \n    \n    \n  </head>\n  <body>\n    <div class=\"mw-page-container\">\n      <div class=\"mw-page-container-inner\">\n        <div class=\"mw-content-container\">\n          <main id=\"content\" class=\"mw-body\">\n            \n            \n            <div id=\"bodyContent\" class=\"vector-body ve-init-mw-desktopArticleTarget-targetContainer\" aria-labelledby=\"firstHeading\" data-mw-ve-target-container=\"\">\n              <div id=\"mwAQ\">\n\n\n\n<p id=\"mwDQ\">A <b id=\"mwDg\">pulled rickshaw</b> (from Japanese <span title=\"Hepburn transliteration\" typeof=\"mw:Transclusion\" id=\"mwDw\"><i lang=\"ja-Latn\">jinrikisha</i></span><span style=\"font-weight:400\" id=\"mwEA\"> (<span title=\"Japanese-language text\"><span lang=\"ja\">人力車</span></span>) 'person/human-powered vehicle'</span>) is a mode of human-powered transport by which a runner draws a two-wheeled cart that seats one or two people.</p>\n\n<p id=\"mwEw\">In recent times the use of human-powered <a rel=\"mw:WikiLink\" href=\"Rickshaw\" title=\"Rickshaw\" id=\"mwFA\">rickshaws</a> has been discouraged or outlawed in many countries due to concern for the welfare of rickshaw workers. Pulled rickshaws have been replaced mainly by cycle rickshaws and <a rel=\"mw:WikiLink\" href=\"Auto_rickshaws\" title=\"Auto rickshaws\" class=\"mw-redirect\" id=\"mwGg\">auto rickshaws</a>.</p>\n</section></div><div><div class=\"zim-footer\">\n    This page is issued from <a class=\"external text\" title=\"Last edited on 2026-03-30\" href=\"https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/?title=Pulled_rickshaw&amp;oldid=1346197634\">Wikipedia</a>. Additional terms may apply for the media files.\n</div>\n</div></div>\n            </div>\n          </main>\n        </div>\n      </div>\n    </div>\n  \n\n</body></html>\n```\n</details>\n\nI'm assuming that Kiwix-Desktop defaults to a character encoding that isn't utf-8 by default. I'm ultimately not planning on using these zims in kiwix-desktop or as anything other than intermediary formats but it got me thinking: does the encoding that kiwix-desktop defaults to make sense? Would it make sense to default to utf-8? What about being able to detect the encoding when it isn't specified?\n\nedit: both chromium and firefox handle the page properly when being served by `kiwix-serve`",

      "body": "## Context\n\nlibkiwix 15 drops `library.xml` support; OPDS becomes the only library file\nformat (kiwix/libkiwix#1377). `Manager::readXml()`, `Library::writeAsXML()`\nand the XML branch of `Manager::readFile()` are removed. kiwix-desktop has to\nread and write its local library as OPDS.\n\n## Current state\n\nThe local library lives in `<dataDir>/library.xml`:\n\n- `Library::Library()` (`src/library.cpp`) loads it with\n  `manager.readFile(\"<dataDir>/library.xml\", false)`.\n- `Library::save()` (`src/library.cpp`) writes it with\n  `mp_library->writeAsXML(\"<dataDir>/library.xml\")`.\n- `KiwixApp::findLibraryDirectory()` (`src/kiwixapp.cpp`) uses the presence of\n  `library.xml` to decide whether `dataDir` is the library directory, and to\n  detect the old pre-release data directory (moving `*.zim` and `library*.xml`\n  from it).\n\nWith libkiwix 15, `writeAsXML()` no longer exists (build failure), and\n`readFile()` rejects an XML file.\n\n## Proposal\n\n- Use `<dataDir>/library.opds` as the local library file:\n  - load it with `Manager::readFile()` (adapting to the libkiwix 15 signature,\n    whose `readOnly`/`trustLibrary` arguments are being dropped);\n  - save it with `Library::writeAsOPDS()`.\n- Update `findLibraryDirectory()` to look for `library.opds` (and keep looking\n  for `library.xml` so existing installations still resolve the same data\n  directory).\n- **No conversion of the old `library.xml`.** As decided in\n  kiwix/libkiwix#1377, the existing file is not migrated: on first start with\n  the new version, the library is rebuilt from the monitored directories\n  (download directory + monitor directory, `KiwixApp::setupDirectoryMonitoring()`),\n  which re-detect the local ZIM files.\n- Leave the old `library.xml` on disk untouched (do not delete it, do not\n  overwrite it), so a user can downgrade or recover data manually.\n- The bookmarks file `library.bookmarks.xml` is a separate format, is still\n  supported by libkiwix 15, and is not affected.\n\n## Consequences for users (to mention in the release notes)\n\nAfter upgrading, the local library is rebuilt from the monitored directories.\nThe following is lost:\n\n- ZIM files that were opened from a location outside the download/monitor\n  directories (they have to be opened again);\n- downloads in progress at the time of the upgrade (see the separate\n  `downloadId` issue);\n- any per-book data that only existed in `library.xml`.\n\n## Tasks\n\n- [ ] `Library::Library()`: read `library.opds`.\n- [ ] `Library::save()`: write `library.opds` with `writeAsOPDS()`.\n- [ ] `KiwixApp::findLibraryDirectory()`: detect `library.opds` (and still\n      `library.xml`); include `library.opds` in the pre-release directory move.\n- [ ] Check that a missing `library.opds` on first start is handled\n      gracefully (empty library, then populated by directory monitoring).\n- [ ] Release notes entry.\n\n## Related\n\n- kiwix/libkiwix#1377: drop `library.xml` support in libkiwix 15\n- kiwix-desktop: keep track of in-progress downloads without relying on\n  `Book::downloadId` in the library file (https://github.com/kiwix/kiwix-desktop/issues/1538)",

      "body": "## Context\n\nlibkiwix 15 drops `library.xml` support; OPDS becomes the only library file\nformat (kiwix/libkiwix#1377). The local OPDS dump does not store `downloadId`,\nand there is no plan to add it. The decision there is that this is a reader-level\nfeature gap, to be solved in kiwix-desktop.\n\n## Problem\n\nkiwix-desktop currently persists the state of running downloads by storing\nthe downloader's ID in the book entry of the local library, which is saved to\n`<dataDir>/library.xml`:\n\n- `ContentManager::startDownload()` (`src/contentmanager.cpp`) calls\n  `book.setDownloadId(downloadId)`, adds the book to the local library with a\n  `.beingdownloadedbykiwix` path suffix, and saves the library.\n- On startup, `DownloadManager::restoreDownloads()` (`src/downloadmanagement.cpp`)\n  iterates over the local library and restores every book whose `downloadId`\n  is non-empty (including the libtorrent case, detected by the ID prefix).\n- `downloadId` is also used as the \"this book is still being downloaded\" flag:\n  - `Library::getBookFilePath()` (`src/library.cpp`)\n  - `ContentManager::getBookInfos()` / `getBookState()` (`src/contentmanager.cpp`)\n  - `DownloadManager` pause/resume/cancel lookups\n- On completion or cancellation, `downloadId` is reset to `\"\"` and the library\n  is saved again.\n\nOnce the library is written as OPDS, `downloadId` is lost on the next save,\nso after a restart:\n\n- running/paused downloads are not restored;\n- partially downloaded books appear in the local library as if they were\n  complete (or as broken entries pointing to `*.beingdownloadedbykiwix`).\n\n## Proposal\n\nPersist the download state in kiwix-desktop itself instead of in the library\nfile, e.g. a small `bookId → downloadId` map stored in the data directory\n(a dedicated file such as `downloads.json`, or a group in the existing\n`QSettings`-based `kiwix-desktop.session`).\n\n- Write the entry when a download starts, and remove it on completion or\n  cancellation (the places that currently call `setDownloadId()`).\n- `restoreDownloads()` reads that map instead of scanning the library.\n- The \"is being downloaded\" checks query the map (or `DownloadManager`)\n  instead of `Book::getDownloadId()`.\n\nThe book entry itself can stay in the local library as it does today. Only the\ndownload ID moves out.\n\n## Related\n\n- kiwix/libkiwix#1377: drop `library.xml` support in libkiwix 15\n- The library file itself also has to move from `library.xml` to OPDS\n  (`Library::Library()` / `Library::save()` in `src/library.cpp`, plus\n  `findLibraryDirectory()` in `src/kiwixapp.cpp`). That is tracked separately.",

      "body": "## Description\n\nKiwix Desktop currently does not have a developer-facing document that explains the overall architecture and the main flows through the application.\n\nFor someone new to the codebase, it can take some time to understand how the main components connect and how a user action flows through the application.\n\nFor example, the following flows are not currently documented in one place:\n\n* Application startup: `main.cpp → KiwixApp → MainWindow`\n* Main UI components and their responsibilities\n* Opening a ZIM file\n* `WebView → zim:// → UrlSchemeHandler → libzim`\n* The roles of Kiwix Desktop, `libkiwix`, `libzim`, and the ZIM file\n\n## Proposed solution\n\nAdd an architecture/contributor guide to the `kiwix-desktop` repository that gives new contributors a high-level understanding of the codebase and points them to the relevant components and source files.\n\nThe guide could cover:\n\n1. Overall architecture\n2. Main components and their responsibilities\n3. Application startup flow\n4. Opening and displaying a ZIM\n5. The `zim://` request flow\n6. A short guide for where to start when investigating common areas of the application\n\nThe documentation should be based on the current implementation and kept focused on helping contributors navigate the codebase.\n",

    {
      "url": "https://api.github.com/repos/kiwix/kiwix-desktop/issues/1503",
      "repository_url": "https://api.github.com/repos/kiwix/kiwix-desktop",
      "labels_url": "https://api.github.com/repos/kiwix/kiwix-desktop/issues/1503/labels{/name}",

      "id": 5329121976,
      "node_id": "I_kwDOBrsij88AAAABPaPyuA",
      "number": 1503,
      "title": "Code review: unvalidated ZIM-triggered navigation reaches OS shell, cross-ZIM XSS in error pages, exceptions as control flow on UI thread",
      "user": {
        "login": "soloturn",
        "id": 825568,
        "node_id": "MDQ6VXNlcjgyNTU2OA==",
        "avatar_url": "https://avatars.githubusercontent.com/u/825568?v=4",
        "gravatar_id": "",
        "url": "https://api.github.com/users/soloturn",
        "html_url": "https://github.com/soloturn",
        "followers_url": "https://api.github.com/users/soloturn/followers",

        "gists_url": "https://api.github.com/users/soloturn/gists{/gist_id}",
        "starred_url": "https://api.github.com/users/soloturn/starred{/owner}{/repo}",
        "subscriptions_url": "https://api.github.com/users/soloturn/subscriptions",
        "organizations_url": "https://api.github.com/users/soloturn/orgs",
        "repos_url": "https://api.github.com/users/soloturn/repos",

        "type": "User",

      "labels": [],
      "state": "open",
      "locked": false,
      "assignees": [],
      "milestone": null,
      "comments": 0,
      "created_at": "2026-09-02T20:55:15Z",
      "updated_at": "2026-09-02T20:55:15Z",
      "closed_at": null,
      "assignee": null,
      "author_association": "NONE",
      "issue_field_values": [],
      "type": null,
      "active_lock_reason": null,
      "sub_issues_summary": {
        "total": 0,
        "completed": 0,

      "issue_dependencies_summary": {
        "blocked_by": 0,
        "total_blocked_by": 0,
        "blocking": 0,        "total_blocking": 0

      "body": "> **AI-assisted review.** Filed by agent driven by @soloturn via [GDD](https://siliconsaga.github.io/yggdrasil/gdd/).\n\nReviewed all `src/*.cpp`/`*.h` for performance, memory consumption, simplification, and error-proneness, weighted toward the first two. Navigation-hijack findings on the untrusted-ZIM-content boundary ranked first.\n\n## Navigation-hijack / untrusted-content boundary\n\n1. `src/webpage.cpp:17-24` — any non-`zim:` navigation from ZIM content is handed straight to `QDesktopServices::openUrl()` with no scheme allowlist, no user-gesture check, no prompt. `acceptNavigationRequest` ignores both `NavigationType` and `isMainFrame`, so a `<meta http-equiv=refresh>`, `location.href=`, hidden `<iframe>`, or auto-submitted form inside a malicious ZIM article silently invokes the OS URL handler for `file:///…/payload.exe`, `smb://attacker/share/x`, `ms-msdt:`, or any registered custom protocol — no click required. `ExternalReqInterceptor` (`kprofile.cpp:164-172`) only blocks non-`zim://` *subresource* requests, never top-level navigations. Fix: allowlist schemes (`http`/`https`/`mailto`), require `NavigationTypeLinkClicked` plus a confirmation dialog before `openUrl`.\n2. `src/webview.cpp:396-401,366-369` — the same unvalidated `m_linkHovered` string (verbatim from `QWebEnginePage::linkHovered`, i.e. ZIM HTML) reaches `QDesktopServices::openUrl()` on Ctrl-click/middle-click and the \"open link in web browser\" context-menu item, tested only with `!startsWith(\"zim://\")`. Same fix as #1, via one shared helper.\n3. `src/urlschemehandler.cpp:250-258,213-238` — unescaped HTML injection into ZIM error pages. `zimEntryPath` is `request->requestUrl().path()` (fully attacker-controlled from a cross-ZIM link); `name`/`path` come from the OPDS-sourced book record. A link from ZIM A to `zim://B.zim/<img src=x onerror=…>` where B is corrupt renders attacker script in **B's origin** — cross-ZIM origin escalation. Fix: `.toHtmlEscaped()` on every interpolated value in `sendHtmlResponse`.\n4. `src/kprofile.cpp:101-119` — downloaded ZIM content is written to a temp file whose extension is taken from the ZIM-supplied filename, then opened via `QDesktopServices::openUrl()`. A ZIM item named `report.hta`/`.desktop`/`.exe` becomes an executed file after \"open\" is picked from a prompt that never shows the actual file type; `setAutoRemove(false)` means these accumulate in temp indefinitely. Fix: allowlist safe extensions, show the resolved filename in the prompt, register the temp file for cleanup.\n\n## Performance\n\n5. `src/multizimbutton.cpp:32-92` + `src/searchbar.cpp:441-442` — `MultiZimButton::updateDisplay()` is wired to `currentTitleChanged`, rebuilding the entire ZIM list (opening/parsing every book file just to check \"can it open\", plus a fresh `ZimItemWidget` per book) on every page title change and tab switch — tens of file opens and PNG decodes on the UI thread per navigation. Fix: rebuild only on `booksChanged`.\n6. `src/library.cpp:76-92` — `Library::getBookIcon()` re-reads and re-decodes the illustration PNG from the ZIM on every call; only the fallback icon is cached. Hit per visible row on every repaint of the suggestion popup (`suggestionlistdelegate.cpp:16`), per bookmark on every `bookmarksChanged`, and via #5. Fix: memoize `QMap<QString, QIcon>` in `Library`, invalidate on `booksChanged`.\n7. `src/urlschemehandler.cpp:38-71,126-180` — all ZIM decompression and all full-text search run synchronously on the UI thread (`requestStarted` is called on the GUI thread; `item.getData(0)` and `searcher->search()`/`getResults()` run inline). A large video item or slow query freezes the whole window. Fix: move to `QtConcurrent`, hold the `QWebEngineUrlRequestJob` until ready.\n8. `src/blobbuffer.cpp:3-7` — every served ZIM item is held in memory twice: `BlobBuffer` keeps `m_blob` alive (which keeps the decompressed cluster alive) *and* `setData()` deep-copies the whole payload into the `QBuffer`'s `QByteArray`. 100MB resident for a 50MB video. Fix: keep one or the other, not both (e.g. `setBuffer` with `QByteArray::fromRawData`).\n9. `src/contentmanager.cpp:128-140,312-332` — `updateModel()` rebuilds `BookInfo` for every book, including re-reading each local book's favicon out of its ZIM, on every `booksChanged` — which fires on **every keystroke** in the library search box via `setSearch()` (`:805-809`). Fix: cache favicon bytes per book id, refresh only on real book change.\n10. `src/contentmanagerview.cpp:28-30` — a 1Hz `QTimer` calls `update()` unconditionally forever, even hidden/idle. Each repaint's per-row state lookup (`ContentManager::getBookState()`, `:399-424`) uses **thrown `std::out_of_range` as normal control flow** (the \"book is remote-only\" path always throws) plus a mutex lock — a C++ exception per row per second, plus every scroll frame. Fix: only run the timer during an active download; give `getBookState` a non-throwing path.\n11. `src/contentmanagerview.cpp:70-83` — `updateSizeHint()` claims to walk visible rows but walks the **entire model** to the last row, called from `resizeEvent` — O(library size) on every frame of a window resize. Fix: stop once past the visible viewport.\n12. `src/kiwixapp.cpp:318-332` — `isCurrentArticleBookmarked()` — `Library::getBookmarks()` returns `const std::vector<kiwix::Bookmark>` **by value** (the top-level `const` also defeats move construction), deep-copying and linear-scanning the entire bookmark list on every title change (wired via `BookmarkButton::update_display`). Fix: return `const std::vector<…>&`, add `Library::isBookmarked(zimId, url)`.\n13. `src/searchbar.cpp:345` — a full `QThread` is heap-allocated and started for each debounced keystroke (100ms debounce still yields up to 10 thread creations/sec of typing); stale results are discarded only *after* the work completes (`m_token` check at `:348`), not cancelled. Fix: one persistent worker/`QThreadPool` with a cancellation flag checked inside `run()`.\n14. `src/contentmanagermodel.cpp:31-53,77-86` — `data()` decodes the favicon PNG on every paint (no `QIcon` cache), and re-queues a duplicate network download on every paint of a not-yet-downloaded thumbnail with no in-flight dedup — scrolling the remote catalog grows an unbounded duplicate-request queue.\n15. `src/tabbar.cpp:198-223,417-505` — `setTitleOf()` calls synchronous `repaint()` rather than `update()`, and `paintEvent` re-measures every tab title with `fontMetrics().boundingRect()` on each pass — title changes fire repeatedly during page load, each forcing a blocking full tab-bar repaint.\n16. `src/mainwindow.cpp:81-82` + `src/webview.cpp:112` — a single title change fans out to: rebuilding two `QMenu`s from scratch (the code's own comment admits it fires too often), #5, #12, plus `WebView::onCurrentTitleChanged` in **every** open tab (each connects to the single global `TabBar` and filters inside the slot). `zimview.cpp:35-70` repeats the same pattern for zoom/save. Fix: connect/disconnect on tab activation.\n\n## Memory / object lifetime\n\n17. `src/webview.cpp:107` — `new KiwixWebChannelObject` with no parent; `QWebChannel::registerObject()` doesn't take ownership — leaks one `QObject` per tab, forever.\n18. `src/webview.cpp:303-320,323-382` — every right-click leaks a `QMenu` plus 2-3 `QAction`s until the tab closes (`contextMenuEvent` calls `exec()` then returns without deleting; the destroyed→deleteLater cleanup only fires if the menu is ever destroyed).\n19. `src/kiwixapp.cpp:242-269` — `printPage()` leaks the `QPrinter` on two paths (dialog cancelled, or `webview` null) and **double-deletes it on a third**: the Qt 6 `printFinished` connection is never disconnected, so printing twice from one tab leaves two live connections firing both lambdas on the second print, the first of which deletes an already-deleted printer.\n20. `src/topwidget.cpp:57-63` — inverted Qt ownership: `menu` (no parent) has its child `toolButton` reparented into the toolbar by `addWidget`, leaving the whole `MainMenu` tree with no parent and no children — leaks.\n21. `src/kiwixapp.cpp:387-390,396-399` — `NextTabAction`/`PreviousTabAction` are each constructed twice via a macro that overwrites the map slot, leaking the first action and silently dropping its shortcut — Ctrl+Tab/Ctrl+Shift+Tab don't work in LTR builds. Fix: use the plural `CREATE_ACTION_SHORTCUTS` macro as `CloseCurrentTabAction` already does.\n22. `src/contentmanagermodel.h:62` / `src/contentmanager.cpp:60` — `m_iconMap` and `mp_remoteLibrary` are unbounded caches with no eviction, bounded only by catalog size.\n\n## Error-proneness\n\n23. `src/tabbar.cpp:276-288` — `closeTabsByZimId()` skips a tab: `closeTab(i)` shifts the next tab into index `i`, but the loop then does `++i`. Deleting a book with two adjacent open tabs leaves one alive, pointing at a just-erased file.\n24. `src/fullscreenwindow.cpp:28-34` + `.h:28` — use-after-free: `m_oldView` is a raw `QWebEngineView*`; the destructor unconditionally dereferences it, but `TabBar::closeTab`/`closeTabsByZimId` can delete that WebView while the fullscreen window is still alive. Fix: `QPointer` + null-check.\n25. `src/contentmanager.cpp:1068-1075` — `handleDisappearedBook()` reads widget-tree state (`getTabWidget()->getTabZimIds()`) from a `QtConcurrent` worker thread — both a Qt \"GUI-thread-only\" violation and a data race on a COW `QString` (`WebView::m_currentZimId`) concurrently written by the UI thread.\n26. `src/contentmanager.cpp:589-597` — `getRemoteOrLocalBook()` returns a reference to an object protected by a mutex that's already released by return; `updateRemoteLibrary` can replace the whole referent from another thread while the caller is still copying from it. Fix: return by value with the lock held.\n27. `src/downloadmanagement.h:38-47` — condition-variable wait uses `if` instead of `while`; a spurious wakeup gives `top()`/`pop()` on an empty `std::priority_queue` — UB.\n28. `src/contentmanager.cpp:284-299` — `getBookTags()` indexes past the end on ordinary ZIM/catalog metadata: `tag[0]` on a possibly-empty string, `splitTag[1]` for any underscore tag without a colon (e.g. a ZIM tagged `_pictures`). Tags come from ZIM metadata + the remote OPDS catalog — untrusted.\n29. `src/kiwixchoicebox.cpp:222-227` — `beautifyString()` does `word[0].toUpper()` with no empty check, called on language/category names from the remote OPDS feed — an empty name is an out-of-bounds write.\n30. `src/settingsmanager.cpp:155-164` — `deducePair()` indexes `pairs[1]` without checking `split('|')` produced two parts; values come from the user-writable config file. `language`/`contentType` lack the guard `initSettings` explicitly applies to `category` for the same legacy format — a hand-edited config crashes at startup.\n31. `src/readinglistbar.cpp:54-72` vs `137-146` — the bookmark list is built with one filter (`getBookmarks()` + archive-openability check) but clicks resolve against a differently-filtered list (`getBookmarks(true)`) — can select the wrong bookmark or throw `std::out_of_range` out of a slot.\n32. `src/kprofile.cpp:48-50` — the file extension is re-appended without its dot (`myfile` → `myfilepdf`, not `myfile.pdf`).\n33. `src/contentmanagerdelegate.cpp:218-237` — `editorEvent()` returns `true` unconditionally, swallowing every event (key presses, mouse presses, hovers) for every item, not just the ones actually handled.\n34. `src/urlschemehandler.cpp:247` — `replyBadZimFilePage()` calls `getBookById()` outside any try/catch, unlike its sibling at `:214-220`; an uncaught `std::out_of_range` can unwind through Qt's event dispatch.\n\n## Simplification\n\n- `src/suggestionlistmodel.cpp:92-101` — `append()` uses `beginResetModel`/`endResetModel` for incremental adds, wiping selection/scroll position on every fetch — the direct cause of the ~50-line scroll-position workaround in `SearchBarLineEdit::onScroll` (`searchbar.cpp:258-307`). Switching to `beginInsertRows`/`endInsertRows` would let most of that be deleted.\n- `src/contentmanager.cpp:983,1017` — `QMap::operator[]` in `const` member functions returns by value, so `const auto& x = m_knownZimsInDir[dir];` silently deep-copies the entire inner map every call. Use `constFind()`/`value()`.\n- `src/contentmanager.cpp:322-326` — `QByteArray::fromRawData` followed by `.detach()` to force a deep copy relies on subtle version-dependent semantics; `QByteArray(data.data(), data.size())` does the same thing unambiguously.\n\n## Minor (verified, lower impact)\n\n- `src/tabbar.cpp:292,305` — off-by-one loop bound (`<=` instead of `<`), harmless only because `widget(count())` returns null.\n- `src/contentmanagermodel.cpp:158-171` — `rootNode` replaced before `beginResetModel()`, and a redundant/invalid `dataChanged` emission right after a reset.\n- `src/webview.cpp:64-67` — unchecked `split('.')[1]` index, currently unreachable only by coincidence.\n- `src/downloadmanagement.cpp:43-54` — off-by-one loop guard reads one past the unit array for values ≥1024⁷.\n- `src/downloadmanagement.cpp:152-164,173` — unsynchronized thread-state field that should be `std::atomic`; worker outlives its owning `ContentManager`.\n- `src/suggestionlistworker.cpp:18,21` — GUI-widget state read from a non-GUI thread, plus an unchecked index.\n- `src/kiwixchoicebox.cpp:191-192,206-208` — `findChild<>()` result dereferenced with no null check.\n- `src/kiwixchoicebox.cpp:29` — a widget parented to the wrong ancestor, outliving its logical owner.\n- `src/kiwixapp.cpp:358` — a comma instead of string concatenation in a `QPixmap` constructor call — the \"off\" icons for reading-list/bookmark toggles never load.\n- `src/contentmanagerview.cpp:15` — `new ContentManagerDelegate()` with no parent; `setItemDelegate` doesn't take ownership.\n- `src/localkiwixserver.cpp:108` — an unchecked index justified only by a comment.\n- `src/library.cpp:162` — download filename taken unfiltered from a remote catalog URL and used as a filesystem path component (`..`/empty both reachable).\n- `src/zimview.cpp:83-90` — a `connect()` with a `this`-capturing lambda and no context object; safe today only incidentally.\n",

      "body": "OS: Windows 11\nVersion: 2.5.1\n\nIssue: The \"Cannot create downloader\" window that pops up when the internet isn't available is creating a bug when a zim is loaded from the command line, but only if the pop-up is closed too quickly.\n\nIf you wait a couple second before hitting OK the launched zim is loaded into a tab with an X button and works properly. But if you hit OK too quickly the X is missing and once you navigate away from the tab (e.g. go to settings) it can't be closed or returned to, although all subsequent tabs work fine.\n\nPossible Solution: Not checking the internet when a local zim is being loaded from the command line or script.  It's not a big deal. One can simply create a loop that detects the pop-up, waits a couple seconds, then closes it. But it would be nice to just load the desired zim from a set of shortcuts without the pop-up and delay for when you just want to quickly look something up.\n\nEdit: Also, I noticed that when this happens the new tab + button is on the left of the loaded tab, while the close X button is where the new tab button normally is (to the right of the tab).\n\n<img width=\"494\" height=\"312\" alt=\"Image\" src=\"https://github.com/user-attachments/assets/a1e7f89a-2920-4558-9380-b3ffdaa0b59a\" />",

The topic is that, at from the time I have loaded this ZIM file in my local library, then everything start to be slow like:\n* Simply filtering on the local library\n* Loading an article in an other ZIM\n\nFor example, if I add a filter on my local library (with around 20 ZIM files) then it takes around 5s whereas if `wikipedia_en_all_maxi_2024-01.zim` is not part of the library, then it takes less than one second.\n\nSame for loading an article which of Wiktionary (which is not stored on the NAS), it takes usually around 1s, but if I have `wikipedia_en_all_maxi_2024-01.zim` in the library, it takes 5s.\n\nConsidering that the two operations listed above should not require reading `wikipedia_en_all_maxi_2024-01.zim`, it seems to me there is a massive waste of hardware resources here.\n\nIt looks like each time the user click somewhere it implies reading `wikipedia_en_all_maxi_2024-01.zim` on the storage backend!?",

      "body": "Currently, the TOC #42 navigations do not go through the Qt system and use Javascript to scroll directly. It would be great to have an **in-page** history, where the history records those actions but sends them to Javascript for scrolling instead of refreshing the current page to the URL.\n\nNote that if a page is refreshed, all DOM states are also reset, which is not what we want.       "body": "Follow up from #1189 and Point 4 of [comment](https://github.com/kiwix/kiwix-desktop/pull/1189#pullrequestreview-2276084711)\n\n**Description:**\nCurrently, If suggestions are displayed, we need to click twice on the suggestions settings to display the overlay. Only one click should be necessary.\n\n**Current behavior:**\nhttps://github.com/user-attachments/assets/93f24799-81bc-456d-8ed9-78c61f11efcd\n\n**Expected click through behavior**\nhttps://github.com/user-attachments/assets/3fad8e56-ab18-4902-969d-401ed841c0d0\n\n**Potential issues and solution discussion:**\n\nQt's popup windows do not provide this fall-through behavior and we will need to implement our own.\n\nOne solution is to make the `QCompleter::popup()` as a `Qt::Tool` window with `setWindowFlags()`, which makes the window non-modal allowing clicks to fall through. The problem comes with:\n1.  how to hide the popup on click.\n2. preventing flickers to the suggestions when typing as seen [here](https://github.com/kiwix/kiwix-desktop/pull/1189#issuecomment-2327980370).\n3. Keep all other suggestion list behavior the same.\n\nDifficulty comes from the focus relationship between `SearchBarLineEdit` and `QCompleter::popup()`.  `QCompleter::popup()` proxies its focus to SearchBarLineEdit and our completion logic relies on focuses. Here is what I have tried:\n\n**1. Disconnect the proxy and handle each separately.** This has deemed difficult as, popup needs focus to allow user keyboard navigations, and it will take focus away from the line edit. In addition, if we hide popup when it focuses out, it will flicker when user starts typing as it constantly races for focus with the line edit. \n\n**2. Keep the proxy and go for eventFilter.** It is ad-hoc and would only work to filter out FocusOut events. Global click events are not reliable for this scenario since multiple clicks can happen at the same time for one click. Also, when users clicks minimize and maximize, the suggestions does not go away as the line edit doesn't lose focus. \n\nThere might be a lot more problems than what I described here when we switch to `Qt::Tool` window. I have not been able to find a solution, so maybe someone more experienced has more knowledge on how to make this work.\n\n",

      "body": "Hello Kiwix team,\r\n\r\nI notice from Kiwix-desktop, is not accessible by Orca the free screen reader under Linux.\r\n\r\nI made a video which shows the behavior of Orca with Kiwix, Kiwix-server and Firefox in narration mode: http://share.emmabuntus.org/Accessibility/Kiwix_vs_Kiwix_server_vs_Firefox_narrate.mp4\r\n\r\nIn this video, we analyze that Orca cannot read the content of the encyclopedia in Kiwix-desktop, while this is possible with Kiwix-server and Firefox.\r\n\r\nSo, it would be good for you to analyze why Orca-desktop content cannot be read by Orca. This may be the same with any other screen reader. If you want to test this, simply install Orca and launch it and open an application to see if it can read its content : https://ncu.libanswers.com/faq/366630\r\n\r\nRegarding Firefox's narration mode, it is implemented by typing Alt+Ctrl+R then pressing N to start reading. Currently, it is the best mode for reading content for blind people because it does not read links, parentheses, and it tracks reading.\r\n\r\nI hope that my comments will help you make Kiwix-desktop accessible to blind people.",

      "body": "For the moment, a new request of type \"https://library.kiwix.org:443/catalog/search?lang=amh&count=-1&tag=_category:gutenberg&notag=_sw:yes\" is done each time a filter is changed in the sidebar. Then everything (book details, no pagination) is downloaded from https://library.kiwix.org. The \"search\" (currently library top bar) is then applied _a posteriori_ on the local result list to have a match.\r\n\r\nI'm not satified because that generates a lot of data transfered (slow and expensive) between local computer and library.kiwix.org.\r\n\r\nI woud like to propose:\r\n* Free text search is handled like any other filter and generates a new API request if changing\r\n* We don't download more book details than what we can display on the screen (but we need to know how many results we have in total). We should use result pagination.\r\n* Libkiwix should cache properly things\r\n  * We should have a cache of requests/response that if we have twice the same requests we identify and avoid downloading twice the results\r\n  * Results list should be handled differently from books. Results reonses should only have the list of book id and book details should be requested (and cached) separatly. That way we can cache book details independently from the API request/response (and save bandwidth).",

      "body": "kiwix-desktop 'master' (830e19bb)\r\n\r\nFound during working on #454\r\n\r\nFrom src/zimview.cpp:\r\n```c\r\nemit mp_tabBar->currentTitleChanged(str);\r\nemit mp_tabBar->currentZimIdChanged(zimId);\r\nemit mp_tabBar->webActionEnabledChanged(QWebEnginePage::Back, mp_webView->isWebActionEnabled(QWebEnginePage::Back));\r\nemit mp_tabBar->webActionEnabledChanged(QWebEnginePage::Forward, mp_webView->isWebActionEnabled(QWebEnginePage::Forward));\r\nemit mp_library->bookmarksChanged();\r\n```\r\n\r\nAccording to Qt documentation, this is a bad practice.\r\nBy signals, only the object itself should notify that its internal state has changed:\r\n\r\nhttps://doc.qt.io/qt-5/signalsandslots.html\r\n\r\n> Signals\r\n> Signals are emitted by an object when its internal state has changed in some way that might be interesting to the object's client or owner. Signals are public access functions and can be emitted from anywhere, but we recommend to only emit them from the class that defines the signal and its subclasses.\r\n",
